# Third-party notices

Molr's original code, documentation, and original artwork are licensed under the
MIT License in `LICENSE`. Third-party code, fonts, assets, and programs retain
their own licenses and are excluded from Molr's MIT grant. The complete original
license texts and notices below accompany the distributions that contain those
components; listing a component does not mean it is present in every artifact.

This file covers the locked npm dependencies, Go modules and standard library,
and the embedded SILK/WASI runtime. npm package metadata alone is not used as a
substitute for license texts. Upstream sources have not been modified by Molr,
except Molr's own separately licensed decoder wrapper, build configuration,
and the app-mark presentation described in the chat channel section below.
Build tools themselves are not included in Molr releases.

## Source availability and licensing choices

- **elkjs 0.9.3 (EPL-2.0):** source is available under EPL-2.0 at
  https://github.com/kieler/elkjs/tree/0.9.3 (commit
  `a8304cf79fde75bc2ab1a89d28320f53f8637436`). Source archive:
  https://github.com/kieler/elkjs/archive/refs/tags/0.9.3.tar.gz .
  Its `build.gradle` documents generation of the JavaScript from ELK Java code
  and GWT dependencies. ELK sources and release history:
  https://github.com/eclipse-elk/elk . EMF and Xtext source references and
  their license notices are supplied in the supplementary section below.
  These portions retain EPL; they are not relicensed as Molr MIT code.
- **DOMPurify:** Molr elects Apache-2.0 from the upstream
  `MPL-2.0 OR Apache-2.0` options. The supplied original license document may
  retain both alternatives.
- **DM Sans and KaTeX fonts:** the fonts retain SIL OFL-1.1, including their
  copyright notices and reserved font names. KaTeX's MIT JavaScript license
  does not replace the font license.
- **SILK SDK:** revision `507be6bca8ce1fb977a061481f1d79e8c610e309` from
  https://github.com/kn007/silk-v3-decoder . Its Skype license expressly does
  not grant patent rights. `silk.wasm` also contains runtime code from WASI
  SDK 34.0; the applicable notices are included below.
- **GitHub mark:** GitHub's artwork and trademarks are not covered by Molr's
  MIT grant. The mark is used for the website's GitHub project link, under
  https://brand.github.com/foundations/logo#legal . No affiliation or
  endorsement is implied.
- **External system programs:** BusyBox and Bubblewrap are not bundled in the
  Molr release archive. The installer checks system dependencies and, when
  authorized, installs them directly through the system package manager.
  Their licenses apply independently. Redistributors adding these binaries
  to a container or other package must meet their distribution obligations.

Dependency versions and review scope are recorded in
`docs/dev/releasing/license-inventory.json` in the source repository. To update the
machine-collected portion, review the dependency changes and run
`npm run notices:update`; the reviewed supplement remains in this same file.

<!-- BEGIN GENERATED NOTICES -->

## Dependency license texts

This is a superset for Molr frontend distributions and Linux/macOS Runtime builds; not every component is present in every artifact. Original licenses apply only to their respective components. Vite/Rolldown notices are included for generated runtime helpers; the build tools themselves are not shipped.

### license-update.mjs

````text
import fs from 'fs';
import path from 'path';

import { fileURLToPath } from 'url';
import { dirname } from 'path';

const __filename = fileURLToPath(import.meta.url);
const __dirname = dirname(__filename);

const year = (new Date()).getFullYear();

const license = `Copyright (c) 2016-${year}, The Cytoscape Consortium.

Permission is hereby granted, free of charge, to any person obtaining a copy of
this software and associated documentation files (the “Software”), to deal in
the Software without restriction, including without limitation the rights to
use, copy, modify, merge, publish, distribute, sublicense, and/or sell copies
of the Software, and to permit persons to whom the Software is furnished to do
so, subject to the following conditions:

The above copyright notice and this permission notice shall be included in all
copies or substantial portions of the Software.

THE SOFTWARE IS PROVIDED “AS IS”, WITHOUT WARRANTY OF ANY KIND, EXPRESS OR
IMPLIED, INCLUDING BUT NOT LIMITED TO THE WARRANTIES OF MERCHANTABILITY,
FITNESS FOR A PARTICULAR PURPOSE AND NONINFRINGEMENT. IN NO EVENT SHALL THE
AUTHORS OR COPYRIGHT HOLDERS BE LIABLE FOR ANY CLAIM, DAMAGES OR OTHER
LIABILITY, WHETHER IN AN ACTION OF CONTRACT, TORT OR OTHERWISE, ARISING FROM,
OUT OF OR IN CONNECTION WITH THE SOFTWARE OR THE USE OR OTHER DEALINGS IN THE
SOFTWARE.`;

fs.writeFileSync(path.join(__dirname, 'LICENSE'), license);
````

### LICENSE-SQLITE

````text
SQLite Is Public Domain

All of the code and documentation in SQLite has been dedicated to the public
domain by the authors. All code authors, and representatives of the companies
they work for, have signed affidavits dedicating their contributions to the
public domain and originals of those signed affidavits are stored in a firesafe
at the main offices of Hwaci. Anyone is free to copy, modify, publish, use,
compile, sell, or distribute the original SQLite code, either in source code
form or as a compiled binary, for any purpose, commercial or non-commercial,
and by any means.

The previous paragraph applies to the deliverable code and documentation in
SQLite - those parts of the SQLite library that you actually bundle and ship
with a larger application. Some scripts used as part of the build process (for
example the "configure" scripts generated by autoconf) might fall under other
open-source licenses. Nothing from these build scripts ever reaches the final
deliverable SQLite library, however, and so the licenses associated with those
scripts should not be a factor in assessing your rights to copy and use the
SQLite library.

All of the deliverable code in SQLite has been written from scratch. No code
has been taken from other projects or from the open internet. Every line of
code can be traced back to its original author, and all of those authors have
public domain dedications on file. So the SQLite code base is clean and is
uncontaminated with licensed code from other projects.
````

### elkjs generated runtime — source and original notices

The website uses the unmodified elkjs 0.9.3 package. The elkjs library and its
ELK algorithms retain EPL-2.0. EMF-GWT portions retain EPL-1.0; Xtext/Xtend
portions retain EPL-2.0. The following sources are made available under those
licenses, with the upstream build files and notices. These licenses do not
replace the MIT license on Molr's separate application code.

  plus the upstream port-placement fix included by elkjs 0.9.3:

  https://github.com/kieler/elkjs/releases/tag/0.9.3 .

## Optional browser component

Molr downloads agent-browser 0.39.0 and Chrome for Testing 149.0.7827.55 directly from their official distribution services when an administrator installs the browser component. These executables are not bundled in the Molr release archive. The installer preserves Chrome package notices and installs the following original agent-browser license alongside its executable. Chrome for Testing is a Google Chrome distribution based on Chromium; its terms and third-party credits remain available in the browser and package.

Source: https://github.com/vercel-labs/agent-browser/tree/v0.39.0

Original license: https://github.com/vercel-labs/agent-browser/blob/v0.39.0/LICENSE

## Docker seccomp policy (Moby)

Copyright The Moby Authors. Apache-2.0.

Source: https://github.com/moby/profiles/tree/4b8214fc4971e06172938a1f1554ccff6647ee80

The original policy is retained in `deploy/docker/vendor/moby-seccomp.json`.
The Molr isolated-container derivative adds constrained `pivot_root`,
`pidfd_getfd`, and `process_vm_readv` rules; guest seccomp remains unchanged.

Desktop packages preserve the matching official Electron distribution LICENSE and LICENSES.chromium.html in resources/licenses, including Chromium/Node.js component notices and source references. These framework files are not part of standalone Molr server distributions. Source: https://github.com/electron/electron/tree/v44.8.0

## Chat channel brand outline icons

`apps/webui/src/interfaces/official/features/ChannelAppIcon.tsx` contains six
brand outline icons identifying the external chat services.

- WeChat, QQ, DingTalk, Telegram and Slack: Tabler Icons v3.49.0,
  `icons/outline/brand-wechat.svg`, `brand-qq.svg`, `brand-dingtalk.svg`,
  `brand-telegram.svg` and `brand-slack.svg`, MIT.
  Source: https://github.com/tabler/tabler-icons/tree/v3.49.0/icons/outline
- Feishu: IconPark, `packages/react/src/icons/NewLark.tsx`, Apache-2.0,
  copyright 2019-present Bytedance Inc.
  Source: https://github.com/bytedance/IconPark/blob/7e34637492ea9f6ae971d1a81e399bff44d1bfa6/packages/react/src/icons/NewLark.tsx

Modifications: SVG paths are embedded in a shared React component. Tabler paths
retain their 24-unit coordinates and 2-unit strokes; IconPark's 48-unit geometry
is scaled by one half, using 4-unit strokes before scaling. Color uses
`currentColor`, with size, padding and background supplied by Molr's theme.
Original icon geometry is retained. No additional npm package is bundled.

The icon code retains the licenses below. The brand names and marks belong to
their respective owners; the icon-library licenses do not grant trademark rights
or imply affiliation or endorsement.
